# computetest — Full Code Audit

**Date:** 2026-05-30  ·  **Scope:** every module (78 audit units; ~12.4k LOC source, ~10k LOC tests)

## Method

Multi-agent orchestration (ultracode): one auditor per module sweeping 12 dimensions → per-finding adversarial verification (each finding an independent skeptic told to *refute* it) → completeness critic → targeted second pass → synthesis into a sequenced fix plan.

- **78 modules** audited · **431 agents** · ~9.4M tokens · ~93 min
- Round 1: 283 findings; critic flagged 12 modules; round 2: +56
- **221 kept** (confirmed/uncertain) · **118 dropped** (refuted / already-handled / intentional)
- Severity: **10 critical**, **42 high**, **66 medium**, **58 low**, **45 nit**
- By dimension: test-quality 47, spec-conformance 43, honesty 33, correctness 30, real-hw-path 26, error-handling 13, edge-case 12, api-consistency 12, type-safety 3, performance 2

## Cross-cutting themes

1. Spec constants/tables/bit-layouts transcribed wrong against the real standard (DDR5 SPD density 8x + ECC byte 13-vs-235; NVMe-MI NMP byte field positions, ROR, invented slot_id; MCTP 0xF0-0xFE reserved band + DSP0239/0236 citations; CXL UE bit map >=12; CXL CciReturnCode.BUSY 0x0009; CXL GET_PARTITION_INFO opcode; NSHSP Kelvin-vs-Celsius; NSS bit positions; lmt sample_count/max_voltage_offset units; QEMU GMER QMP arg set). These are latent-but-shipping wrong values that misparse/mis-encode on real hardware.
2. Tautological/self-referential tests and hand-built corpora: expected values derived from the buggy code or mock rather than the spec, so the suite is green while the code is wrong (SPD corpus, NMP round-trip, CXL UE/RAS, MCTP EID, lmt sample_count, Gage R&R 'pinned to textbook' claim, DDR5 ECC/PPR/RFM, gPTP rate_ratio=1.0, FRER no-wrap, QBV single-window/positive-jitter, GMSL EOM/real-path, CXL poison/maintenance/events).
3. Verdict logic ignores or vacuously satisfies a signal it claims to gate on: empty-iterable all() passes (CXL retimer lanes=[], MSA bootstrap sxy==0 spurious-zero), better-than-target metric never fed to the verdict (Gen6 FBER, GMSL check_eom thresholds), unmeasured/empty/no-source folded to PASS (chain no-error-source, empty device set, all-skip heartbeat, ECS disabled scrub, hot-standby empty TE, ber --bits continue, lmt --via-pci-lmt, CXL events PASS-on-fatal).
4. Truthiness/sign/boundary bugs on physically-valid edge values (gpu_temp 0.0C fallthrough; tegra 0<max_zone false-FAILs sub-zero cold-start; nvme temp 0<temp false-FAILs 0C/missing; CRC/persist_mode/respond_delay negative passes; SPD density/module-type unknown defaults to a valid-looking value).
5. Real-hw subprocess/parsers lack guards the rest of the codebase already applies: unchecked returncode/empty-stdout (ethernet, CAN), unguarded int()/regex (ethernet TDR decimal+multiline, gpu throttle hex, gmsl video sysfs), no read timeout (tegrastats), no exception isolation per check (harness aborts whole plan), int(inf) OverflowError instead of InstrumentError (BERT/TSN counters), non-finite JSON persisted (results.py Infinity).
6. Wrong/shared decode table or LBMS/LABS read at the wrong topology point produces dropped or mislabeled diagnostics (AerSnapshot devstatus fallback decodes via AER table; linkstate reads LBMS/LABS at endpoint; chain misses pre-test downgrade; gmsl per-link reads aggregate sysfs).
7. Documentation/honesty overstatements: docstrings claim behavior the code does not implement (reset RSF=0 contradiction, gmsl channel filter/PoC carve-out, MCTP framing layer, ras.py real backend, ocp_telemetry PASS-on-fatal-strings), and operator-facing notes/labels that could mislead a field engineer.

## Ordered implementation plan

Execute sequentially. `depends_on` = fix that must land first; **BLOCKED** = needs external spec data the project deliberately stubbed.

### 1. [CRITICAL] Fix DDR5 SPD parser: ECC byte, density table, length guard, and unknown-value sentinels

*Modules:* `spd.py`

Decode ECC from JESD400-5 byte 235 bits[4:3] (ecc = bool((data[235] >> 3) & 0x03)), not byte 13. Replace DENSITY_GBIT with the correct per-die JESD400-5 encoding (1=0.5Gb..8=8Gb; store Mbit if sub-Gb granularity matters) and rename density_gbit -> density_gbit_per_die (document it is per-die, not module capacity). Raise the length guard to require len(data) > 235 (full SPD block), distinguishing 'truncated read' from 'not a DDR5 SPD'. Make unknown density and unknown module_type fail-loud: use a None/sentinel for unmapped codes (not a valid-looking 0 / 'unknown' that can silently pass), and have check_spd flag density_unknown/module_type_known.

*Risk:* medium: changes the public SpdInfo field name (density_gbit) and parse_spd contract; downstream to_dict/summary and any consumer must follow. Verify mypy files=src still passes.

### 2. [HIGH] Re-anchor the DDR5 SPD corpus + tests to spec-derived values · depends_on [1]

*Modules:* `test_ddr5_spd.py`, `spd.py`

Replace the synthetic 16-byte spd.hex with a full-length (>=236-byte) image populated at byte 235 (ECC bits) and the correct density code, and hand-derive expected.json from JESD400-5 (NOT from the parser output). Add a negative case where byte 235 ECC bits and byte 13 disagree, plus an unknown-density and unknown-module-type case asserting the new fail-loud behavior. This must be done with the order-1 fix so the previously-tautological corpus actually exercises the spec.

*Risk:* low: test/corpus-only, but the corpus regeneration must be derived from the datasheet, not the code.

### 3. [CRITICAL] Rewrite the NVMe-MI NMP header byte to the spec layout (ROR/NMIMT/CSI) and drop the invented slot_id

*Modules:* `messages.py`

Encode nmp = (ror << 7) | ((message_type & 0x0F) << 3) | (csi & 0x01); decode message_type=(nmp>>3)&0x0F, csi=nmp&0x01, ror=(nmp>>7)&0x01. Add an explicit ror field to MiMessage (0=request, 1=response) and set/parse bit 7. Remove the 2-bit slot_id field entirely (the spec command slot is the single CSI bit). Fix the module docstring (lines 14-21) and the byte-layout comments to match. This is the single root-cause fix covering all four NMP findings (wrong positions, CSI@bit4, missing ROR, invented slot).

*Risk:* high: changes the on-wire encoder/decoder and the MiMessage dataclass shape (adds ror, removes slot_id); every caller and the round-trip test must be updated. Latent today (real MCTP path is NotImplementedError) so no live regression, but it is the authoritative wire format.

### 4. [MEDIUM] Pin NVMe-MI NMP encoding to a spec-derived golden byte vector · depends_on [3]

*Modules:* `test_nvme_mi.py`

Replace the self-consistent encode/decode round-trip with (a) a golden-vector test asserting the exact NMP byte against the spec value for a known (message_type, csi, ror) tuple, and (b) a decode test against a hand-built spec-conformant frame (e.g. a response with ROR=1). Remove/fix the 'UNH-IOL v25 test 5.1' label so it does not claim conformance the round-trip never verified.

*Risk:* low: test-only, but the golden value must come from the spec/libnvme, not from the new encoder.

### 5. [HIGH] Fix CXL spec constants: UE bit map, CciReturnCode.BUSY, GET_PARTITION_INFO opcode

*Modules:* `ras.py`, `mailbox.py`

In ras.py CXL_UE_BITS, remap Internal_Error->bit 14, IDETxError->bit 15, IDERxError->bit 16, leaving bits 12-13 reserved/undefined (bits 0-11 stay). In mailbox.py set CciReturnCode.BUSY=0x0006 and GET_PARTITION_INFO=0x4100 (optionally add IDENTIFY_MEMORY_DEVICE=0x4000); cross-check the other 4xxx opcodes.

*Risk:* low-medium: ras UE remap changes a shared decode table used by both Mock and Real backends and emit; verdict (raw!=0) is unaffected but decoded names/telemetry change.

### 6. [HIGH] Re-anchor CXL RAS/UE and emit tests to the spec bit positions · depends_on [5]

*Modules:* `test_cxl_ras.py`, `test_cxl_emit.py`

Rewrite assertions to spec positions: InternalError=bit14, IDETxError=bit15, IDERxError=bit16, and add a bit-16 case so a dropped/mislabeled high bit fails the suite. These tests currently lock in the off-by-two mapping and would block the order-5 fix, so they must change together.

*Risk:* low: test-only.

### 7. [CRITICAL] Fix CXL coherency Type-1 false-FAIL and de-tautologize the Type-1 test

*Modules:* `device.py`, `test_cxl_device.py`

Gate coherency_bridging on device-coherent memory, not cache: require (hdm_d_capable and bi_snoop_capable) only when mem_capable (TYPE2; TYPE3 under HDM-DB) else True. Update the docstring from 'a cache-capable device must expose HDM-D[B]' to 'a device-memory-coherent device'. Change test_type1_cache_only to the realistic case hdm_d_capable=False, bi_snoop_capable=False asserting h.ok; keep the Type-2 absent-bridging FAIL test.

*Risk:* medium: changes a conformance verdict; ensure TYPE2/TYPE3 paths still gate correctly.

### 8. [HIGH] Fix the QEMU CXL GMER injection to send all required QMP fields and validate against the schema

*Modules:* `cxl_inject.py`, `test_cxl_qmp_inject.py`

Add descriptor, type, transaction_type (as 'transaction-type'), sub_type (as 'sub-type') to inject_general_media_event (build the args dict explicitly so hyphenated keys are sent), and expose the General-Media optionals as needed. Pin a query-qmp-schema snapshot (or a version assert) and reconcile every cxl-inject-* arg list against it; note the verified QEMU version in the docstring (discharges the AUDIT NOTE). Make FakeQMPServer validate required members against the pinned QEMU schema so a missing field fails the test the way real QEMU does. Assert the complete required-arg set per command.

*Risk:* medium: blocked on having the exact QEMU 11.0 query-qmp-schema field list, which the project intends to pin from the running emulator (sim helper, not src).

### 9. [HIGH] Fix NVMe-MI subsystem/reset spec correctness (NSHSP temperature units, NSS bit map, Reset Type enum, operator note)

*Modules:* `subsystem_health.py`, `reset.py`

> **BLOCKED:** NSS exact P0LA/P1LA bit indices and the precise Reset Type table need the ratified NVMe-MI 2.1 spec, which returned HTTP 403 during verification; the load-bearing corrections (Celsius not Kelvin; Drive Functioning not bit 0; only 00h Reset Type) are confirmed and can proceed, but the remaining bit indices must be reconciled against the obtained spec.

subsystem_health: parse NSHSP Composite Temperature as signed int8 Celsius (two's-complement: temp_c = byte-256 if byte>=128 else byte), drop the Kelvin field/label/-273 conversion (reserve Kelvin only for the in-band SMART log); cross-check NSS bit positions against the targeted NVMe-MI revision (Drive Functioning is not bit 0) and quote the table cell, adding a decode test fed a real captured byte. reset: rename the field to 'Reset Type' citing the correct figure (only 00h defined; 01h-FFh reserved), drop/correct the non-existent SUBSYSTEM_RESET_INHIBIT value, and fix the header+operator note so the subsystem-reset default is NOT presented as leaving host I/O untouched. Update the mock and the (currently self-consistent) tests to match.

*Risk:* medium: real decode/encode paths are NotImplementedError today (latent), but these are the spec-of-record values and the mock + tests encode the wrong ones.

### 10. [CRITICAL] Fix the two TSN critical verdict bugs (QBV guard band + FRER sequence wrap) and their masking tests

*Modules:* `qbv.py`, `frer.py`, `test_tsn_qbv.py`, `test_tsn_frer.py`

QBV: compute the frame deadline as the end of the contiguous run of consecutive GCL entries for which the TC gate stays open (walk forward summing durations from the window covering start, until the first entry where gate_states & (1<<tc) is clear; handle cycle wrap), and compare start+tx_ns against that gate-close time instead of the single window's w_end. Also change edge-jitter to max(abs(j)) to bound both early and late excursions (mirror gptp.py |TE|). FRER: model the sequence space modulo 2^seq_width (default 16): mask incoming seq to the width and use signed modular distance for the in-window staleness test and the high-water advance. Add the missing tests: a TC open across two consecutive entries (no guard violation), a large-negative jitter sample (edge_jitter_ok False), and a wrapping replicated 65534->1 stream asserting exactly-once OK; also make the dead 'no_duplicates_delivered' check meaningful or remove it.

*Risk:* high: core verdict logic in two conformance analyzers; the contiguous-run walk must correctly handle cycle wrap. Tests currently green must flip then pass.

### 11. [HIGH] Fix remaining TSN spec/correctness defects (preemption 0.8x window, gPTP rate-ratio, hot-standby)

*Modules:* `preemption.py`, `gptp_proto.py`, `gptp_hotstandby.py`, `test_tsn_preemption.py`, `test_tsn_gptp_proto.py`, `test_tsn_hotstandby.py`

preemption: change the Respond gate to respond_delay_ms <= verify_time_ms (drop the non-spec 0.8x) or rename the constant to RESPOND_MARGIN_FRACTION and stop labeling it the Clause-99 criterion in docstring + coverage matrix; reject negative respond_delay; require >=2 fragments for a 'fragmented' frame; emit per-failure reason strings into notes; add a boundary test pinning the constant. gPTP: change mean_link_delay_ns to (rate_ratio*(t4-t1) - (t3-t2))/2 and fix the docstring; add a Pdelay test at rate_ratio=1.0+200e-6; add offsetScaledLogVariance to AnnounceMsg + BMCA key (or document the simplification). hot-standby: align the TE metric and its budget label (one-sided max|TE| vs peak-to-peak — pick one, ideally report both like gptp.py), and raise/flag on an empty te_samples_ns window instead of PASSing 0ns.

*Risk:* medium: changes conformance verdicts (preemption false-FAIL direction, gPTP number at non-unity ratio). Several tests bake in the wrong values and must be updated.

### 12. [CRITICAL] Fix the MSA Deming bootstrap spurious-zero false PASS and small-n coverage

*Modules:* `station_correlation.py`, `test_msa.py`

Skip degenerate resamples instead of treating slope=0 as a fit: raise inside _deming_point on the sxy==0 branch (let the existing except ZeroDivisionError: continue catch it) or add 'if sxy == 0: continue' in the bootstrap loop, and require a minimum count of valid resamples before reporting a CI (this also removes the inflated se_slope/se_intercept at small n). Add regression tests at n=3 with a pure scale factor asserting not agrees / not ok and that the slope CI does not collapse to (0, slope). Separately, use a t_{0.975,n-1} quantile for ci95_bias in bland_altman (keep 1.96 for the limits-of-agreement).

*Risk:* medium: changes the station-agreement verdict at the documented minimum n; ensure non-degenerate cases are unchanged.

### 13. [CRITICAL] Fix the NVMe self-test-log critical wrong-key false PASS and the temp false-FAIL, and add a real corpus replay

*Modules:* `health.py`, `test_parsers_corpus.py`, `nvme`

> **BLOCKED:** Exact nvme-cli self-test-log JSON key strings/casing should be pinned from a real `nvme self-test-log -o json` capture (no corpus exists in-tree); the corrected keys are derived from nvme-cli source but must be confirmed against the deployed version and committed as a corpus fixture.

In _real_self_test_log read the actual nvme-cli keys ('List of Valid Reports' array, 'Current Device Self-Test Operation', 'Current Device Self-Test Completion', per-entry 'Self test result' — exact casing) and do NOT default a missing result/array to a pass: if the expected keys are absent, raise rather than return passed=True. Mask the operation nibble: in_progress = (cur & 0xF) != 0 (drop the bogus 0xF idle). Default percent to 0 when unknown. Separately split the temperature gate: require the temperature key present and gate as smart.get('temperature',0) <= max_temp_c (no 0< lower bound) so a 0C/sub-zero/missing reading is N/A or fail-present, not a false over-temp FAIL. Capture a real (or spec-worked) self-test-log.json (one passing, one failing) into corpus/nvme and add a parametrized replay asserting result/in_progress/passed through the real path.

*Risk:* high: wrong-PASS on a real device self-test is the worst case; key names need confirmation against the installed nvme-cli version.

### 14. [CRITICAL] Harden Ethernet/CAN real-hw parsing and subprocess handling (TDR critical + returncode + role)

*Modules:* `ethernet.py`, `ethtool`

> **BLOCKED:** Authentic `ethtool --cable-test-tdr` and master-slave cfg!=status output should be captured into corpus for a faithful replay; the netlink multi-line layout is reconstructed from spec/driver behavior and needs a real capture to pin exactly.

Rewrite _real_cable_test to parse ethtool --cable-test-tdr line-by-line (accumulate the last pair/code, pair it with the next 'fault length' line; capture distance as \d+(?:\.\d+)? for fractional metres; treat OK/Good Cable as non-fault) so faults on separate lines are not missed and 2.31m is not read as 31m. Inspect .returncode on all ethtool/ip subprocess.run calls and on non-zero raise (or record a distinct 'error'/'unknown' verdict) so a tool failure is never reported as a clean link/CAN PASS — mirror the lmt_adapter/bert returncode-check convention. Match the negotiated 'master-slave status:' line specifically for role (fall back to cfg only if status absent). Normalize the cable-test pair field type (str letter) across mock and real. Add a real --cable-test-tdr corpus capture (and a cfg!=status case) so these real branches are replayed.

*Risk:* high: three independent real-hw defects in one untested pragma path; the multiline TDR parser is the trickiest. Add corpus replays to lock it in.

### 15. [HIGH] Fix the AerSnapshot devstatus-fallback decode (shared root cause for empty uncorrectable + 'RxErr' mislabel)

*Modules:* `aer.py`, `test_aer_edges.py`

Give AerSnapshot a source field (default 'aer') like ErrorReading and select DEVSTATUS_*_BITS when source=='devstatus'; set source from read_errors().source in the snapshot() no-AER fallback. This single fix resolves both the empty uncorrectable name list and the bit-0 'RxErr' mislabel. Strengthen the regression test to assert the decoded name list ([n for _,n,_ in snap.uncorrectable] == ['NonFatalDetected']) and a correctable analogue, not just has_uncorrectable.

*Risk:* low-medium: shared decode path used by diagnostics and OCP emit; verdict (raw!=0) unaffected but reason strings/telemetry change.

### 16. [HIGH] Fix linkstate/diagnostics topology-aware downgrade detection (LBMS/LABS at downstream port + chain max fallback + no-source skip)

*Modules:* `linkstate.py`, `diagnostics.py`

In check_link/diagnose, read/clear LBMS/LABS at the link-owning downstream port (resolve via link_chain()/read_port_type(), mirroring diagnose_chain) or gate bw_changed on read_port_type(bdf) being a DOWNSTREAM_PORT and document the endpoint limitation. In diagnose_chain, when expected_speed/expected_width are None, fall back to the downstream port's max_link_speed/max_link_width (backend.get_device(li.downstream_bdf)) so a pre-test degraded link is caught. Capture the per-BDF error_source once and, when it is 'none', mark the chain segment status 'skip' (add 'skip' to the documented set) and fold an all-skip/empty chain through to skip (mirror the single-device honesty rule); also make diagnose with an empty device set return EXIT_UNAVAIL with a warning rather than PASS.

*Risk:* medium: touches the core PCIe link-degradation verdict and chain aggregation; coordinate the source-capture refactor with the skip handling.

### 17. [HIGH] Feed Gen6 post-FEC FBER into the BERT verdict (or correct the overclaiming docstring)

*Modules:* `bert.py`, `test_bert_fec.py`

When FEC stats are present, compare fec.fber_estimate (or a confidence-bounded BER from post_fec_flit_errors) against the known Gen6 FBER target and set verdict.status='fail' / surface it through .ok and the CLI exit, instead of only emitting a prose note. If AER-only verdicts on Gen6 are genuinely intended, change the _GEN6_FEC_EXPLANATION text so it stops claiming FBER is 'the actual reliability signal' while ignoring it. Add a test asserting the verdict (not just fber_estimate > 0) for an over-target FBER.

*Risk:* medium: changes the Gen6 pass/fail decision; AER uncorrectable latch remains a backstop.

### 18. [HIGH] Fix the PCIe topology/harness/results robustness defects (bdf match crash, per-check isolation, non-finite JSON)

*Modules:* `topology.py`, `harness.py`, `results.py`

topology: coerce only numeric match keys (leave bdf a string) in load_config; use a single base-0 coercion helper in both load_config and matches(); add a round-trip load_config bdf test; annotate when a non-leaf BDF reports PORT_ENDPOINT (cap-walk failure). harness: wrap each per-target functional check (and the step-2/step-4 diagnose calls) in try/except recording a skip TestRecord, mirroring _try_margin, so one bad device/handle cannot abort the station run; emit a terminal heartbeat in a finally; compute the heartbeat detail with the same three-way logic as _verdict_exit_any (PASS only if no fail and no skip). results: serialize non-finite floats as null/sentinel before persisting (json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False) with pre-conversion, or BertResult.to_dict() emitting None for inf) so stored JSON is RFC-8259 conformant and the dashboard does not 500.

*Risk:* medium: harness isolation must not swallow genuine failures (record skip with the exception, do not pass); results serialization change affects DB round-trip and dashboard.

### 19. [HIGH] Harden the instrument SCPI overflow paths and remaining real-hw parsers (BERT/TSN counters, gpu throttle, gmsl video, tegra)

*Modules:* `instruments.py`, `gpu.py`, `video.py`, `tegra.py`

instruments: add a finite-check helper used by ExternalBERT.read_result (error count and bits) and TSNTrafficGenerator.read_counters so a 9.9E37->inf/nan sentinel raises InstrumentError (not OverflowError), and treat a non-finite bits count as a fault (raise or NaN) instead of letting errors/inf collapse to ber=0.0. gpu: route the throttle hex field through the same try/except helper as _int/_float so [Not Supported]/[N/A]-style tokens degrade to 0 rather than crashing the GPU verdict. gmsl/video: wrap --get-fmt-video in try/except subprocess.TimeoutExpired (treat timeout/empty as w=h=0); guard the sysfs int() parses (memory.py _read_int pattern, len(parts)>=2); derive frame_sync from a real FSYNC/line-valid signal or set frame_sync_ok=None and skip rather than reusing all-locked; read per-link sysfs (or mark locked/error_count as derived-from-aggregate). tegra: bound _collect_tegrastats with a wall-clock deadline / select-poll; broaden _RAIL_RE to tolerate the JetPack 5/6 'NNNNmW/NNNNmW' format; resolve gpu_temp against the offline-filtered meaningful dict with explicit 'GPU' in membership (fixes both the -256C surfacing and the 0.0C falsy fallthrough); track a have_data flag and gate as have_data and max_zone <= max_temp_c (drop the 0< lower bound) so sub-zero cold-start readings are not false-FAILed.

*Risk:* medium: all real-hw (# pragma) paths the mock cannot catch; add corpus/fixture replays where practical (tegra Orin mW line, gmsl per-link sysfs, ethtool TDR already in step 14).

### 20. [HIGH] Fix GMSL eye/shmoo verdict bugs (check_eom thresholds, empty vertical_mv, shmoo lock-by-field + settle)

*Modules:* `checks.py`, `shmoo.py`, `test_gmsl_eom.py`, `test_gmsl_shmoo.py`

check_eom: honor per-instance EOM thresholds via serdes.eom_verdict(eom) (matching shmoo) or pass ui_min=serdes._eom_ui_min/mv_min=serdes._eom_mv_min; guard height_mv=max(eom.vertical_mv) against an empty list (or raise SerDesError). shmoo: look up lock by .link field (next(ll for ll in lock_status() if ll.link==link), raise SerDesError if absent) instead of positional index; after set_temperature poll chamber.settled(tolerance_c=...) with a bounded timeout (and optionally verify psu.measure_voltage()) before force_relock/read so eye and die_temp are captured at the intended V/T corner; fetch lock_status once per corner. Add a check_eom test at a tightened threshold (70mV eye, mv_min=100 -> eye_open False) and a shmoo settle/lock-order test.

*Risk:* medium: changes a GMSL gate (tightened-threshold PASS) and shmoo timing on real hardware; the settle poll needs a sane bounded timeout.

### 21. [HIGH] Fix CXL retimer vacuous PASS and the remaining CXL verdict/emit honesty gaps

*Modules:* `retimer.py`, `emit.py`, `hdm.py`, `test_cxl_retimer.py`

retimer: materialize the lane list and reject empty (raise, or set all_lanes_eye_open=False) so lanes=[] cannot vacuously pass; add a lanes=[] regression test; optionally default eye thresholds from the instance for symmetry with tj_max. emit: drive emit_cxl_events from record severity (fail/UNKNOWN-SKIP when any r.log >= FAILURE) or emit a neutral diagnosis rather than a hardcoded PASS, and add a FATAL-record test; in emit_cxl_ras fall back to naming the raw ue bitmask when the decoded list is empty so a FAIL always carries a cause. hdm: fix the calc_interleave_pos docstring + test comment to state endpoint-first ordering (matching the kernel recurrence). Add a flit_speed_consistent sanity check (256B flit below 32 GT/s = inconsistent).

*Risk:* low-medium: retimer empty-lane is a wrong PASS only on explicit lanes=[]; emit changes affect the ocp-diag stream not the primary verdict.

### 22. [HIGH] Fix the lmt schema-portability spec values (sample_count inversion + max_voltage_offset units) and DDR5 lmt flags

*Modules:* `lmt_adapter.py`, `lmt.py`, `test_lmt_adapter.py`, `test_ddr5_lmt.py`

lmt_adapter: emit sample_count as the raw 7-bit register value (0..127) and sample_count_bits=int(2**(sample_count/3)) per the OCP pci_lmt reference (fix the inversion and the dropped /3); express max_voltage_offset in 0.01V register units (e.g. 49) and relabel the field/comment '0.01V units', converting from mV when emitting (mirror max_timing_offset). ddr5/lmt: set ind_left_right_timing=False and ind_up_down_voltage=False (a single combined eye-to-failure per axis is emitted, matching from_margin_result/gmsl). Add tests pinning the OCP sample_count<->sample_count_bits relationship, the max_voltage_offset range, and the DDR5 ind_* flag values; document/guard the FORWARD vs REVERSE GMSL EOM record collision (encode direction into a distinguishing column or document non-concatenation).

*Risk:* low-medium: informational schema metadata, not verdict-flipping, but downstream OCP/BigQuery consumers depend on it; tests currently mirror the buggy values.

### 23. [MEDIUM] Fix the CLI/ocp-diag verdict-honesty and error-mapping defects

*Modules:* `cli.py`, `ocpdiag.py`

ber --bits: map the assess status to an exit code (pass->EXIT_PASS, continue->EXIT_UNAVAIL, fail->EXIT_FAIL) and pass v.status into the ocp-diag diagnosis/step_end instead of hardcoding 'pass'. lmt --via-pci-lmt: parse the returned per-lane records and map a failing lane to EXIT_FAIL, or stop emitting a gate-trustable PASS and document that the exit code only reflects that the tool ran. Move _open_ocpdiag inside main's try (or wrap the open) mapping OSError->EXIT_IO with a clean message; add a bad-path test asserting EXIT_IO and no traceback. Give run_end an explicit status arg and map setup/IO/not-found/interrupt exit codes (2/3/4/130) to NOT_APPLICABLE with testStatus=ERROR (the only non-FAIL spec-legal run result), so a test-program fault is not recorded as a DUT FAIL; keep COMPLETE/PASS-FAIL only for real verdicts. Validate run_log/step_log severity against the OCP enum.

*Risk:* medium: changes CLI exit-code contract and ocp-diag run-result semantics that CI gates and MT pipelines key on; update docs/reference/cli.md and ocp-diag-emitter.md accordingly.

### 24. [MEDIUM] Fix the remaining medium correctness/edge/error-handling defects across subsystems

*Modules:* `margining.py`, `memory.py`, `fixtures.py`, `aem.py`, `vpd.py`, `ecs.py`, `rfm.py`, `calibration_registry.py`

margining: seed the mock worst lane within the actual width (worst_lane = h[1] % max(1,n)) so marginal x4/x8 links can fail. memory: add a legacy-EDAC csrow/ch fallback glob (or flag when controller CE>0 but per_dimm is empty) so the per-DIMM concentration check is not a silent no-op. fixtures: raise InstrumentError on an empty IDN prefix; build open_all in a try/except that closes already-opened instruments before re-raising; make the no-PyYAML JSON fallback re-raise an actionable 'install pyyaml' error; use a context manager + encoding on the file read. aem: track last-emit time per event type (dict keyed by AsyncEventType) so coalescing is per-type on interleaved streams; add the interleaved-within-window test. vpd: when mock is explicitly False and transport is None, raise instead of returning fabricated mock FRU data (apply the same across the mi/ package). ecs: gate on scrub.enable_background and 0 < scrub_time_s <= max_scrub_time_s so a disabled/zero-duration scrub fails. rfm: store the raw rfm_enabled/prac_enabled (or emit None/'n/a' for not-required) instead of overwriting the observed value with True. calibration_registry: normalize datetime down to date in _to_date (return v.date() when isinstance datetime); fix the JSON-fallback error message; use a context manager on the read.

*Risk:* medium: independent self-contained fixes; each touches a verdict or a real-hw config path. Can be split into per-module commits.

Also fix the docstring/__all__ nits (cxl/__init__, gmsl/__init__ DEFAULT_*, nvme/mi/__init__, gpu XID 92 label, retimer replay semantics, mctp/messages framing docstrings, FakeQMPServer stale comment).

## Findings by severity

### Critical (10)

**C1. TDR distance-to-fault is corrupted for any non-integer length (real hardware always reports fractional metres)**  
`src/computetest/ethernet.py` · ethernet.py:149-151 (_real_cable_test) · _real-hw-path_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** The regex group `(\d+)\s*m` captures only the digits AFTER the decimal point of a distance like `2.31m`, so a fault truly at 2.31 m is reported as 31.0 m (and 2.30m -> 30.0m). The distance-to-fault — the entire point of a TDR cable test — is silently wrong on real hardware.  
- **Evidence:** for m in re.finditer(r"pair (\w).*?(open|short|impedance).*?(\d+)\s*m", out):
    faults.append({"pair": m.group(1), "code": m.group(2), "distance_m": float(m.group(3))})  — verified: input 'pair b code open circuit, fault length: 2.31m' yields distance_m=31.0  
- **Fix:** Capture a full decimal: change the third group to `(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)` so `2.31m` -> 2.31. The mock test uses 2.3 and never exercises this because the mock path doesn't go through this regex.  
- **Verifier:** Confirmed real defect in a real-hw path. At ethernet.py:149-151, _real_cable_test parses ethtool --cable-test-tdr output with `r"pair (\w).*?(open|short|impedance).*?(\d+)\s*m"`. The distance group `(\d+)` cannot capture a decimal. Real ethtool prints fault length with two decimals (%0.2fm, e.g. "2.31m"), so a decimal is the normal real-hardware output. I reproduced the claimed symptom: for a line "pair b code open circuit, fault length: 2.31m" (lowercased), `(\d+)` captures only "31" -> distance_m=31.0 (and 2.30m -> 30.0). The author clearly knows distances are fractional: _SPEED_RE at line 16 already uses `(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)`, yet the TDR regex does not — so this is an oversight, not intentional. No test exercises this path (grep for _real_cable_test/finditer/cable-test-tdr in tests/ finds nothing) and the mock path (_mock_cable_test) returns a hardcoded dict, bypassing the regex entirely, so the bug is invisible to the suite. The suggested fix `(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)` is correct and yields 2.31. One nuance the finding omits (does not refute it): the code uses re.finditer WITHOUT re.DOTALL, so `.` won't cross newlines; in the authentic modern netlink layout where the pair-code line and fault-length line are SEPARATE, the regex matches nothing -> faults=[] -> status="ok", i.e. a real fault is reported as PASS. So across plausible real ethtool layouts the result is either a corrupted distance (same-line) or a missed fault / false PASS (separate-line) — both severe. Severity stays critical: a TDR test's entire purpose is distance-to-fault, and on real hardware this either silently mislocates the fault or produces a wrong PASS verdict, in an untested pragma real-hw path that ships to the field.  

**C2. TDR parser misses real faults: kernel prints pair-code and fault-length on SEPARATE lines and `.` does not cross newlines**  
`src/computetest/ethernet.py` · ethernet.py:149 (_real_cable_test) · _real-hw-path_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** Real `ethtool --cable-test-tdr` output puts the result code (e.g. 'Pair B code Open Circuit') and the distance ('Pair B, fault length: 2.31m') on different lines. Without re.DOTALL the `.*?` between the code token and the distance cannot span the newline, so finditer finds no match and the function returns status='ok' with faults=[] — a faulted cable is reported as a healthy link (false PASS of cable_ok).  
- **Evidence:** Verified against realistic multiline output: 'pair b code open circuit\npair b, fault length: 2.31m' -> finditer yields [] (no faults). The regex assumes code+distance share one line, but the kernel netlink TDR formatter emits them separately.  
- **Fix:** Parse per-pair across lines: either add re.DOTALL and anchor per pair, or scan line-by-line accumulating the last seen pair/code and pairing it with the next 'fault length' line; treat 'OK'/'Good Cable' codes as not-a-fault. Add a corpus capture of real --cable-test-tdr output so this real branch is actually replayed.  
- **Verifier:** Independently reproduced. ethernet.py:149 uses `re.finditer(r"pair (\w).*?(open|short|impedance).*?(\d+)\s*m", out)` WITHOUT re.DOTALL. Real `ethtool --cable-test-tdr` (netlink cable-test formatter) emits the pair result code and the fault length from distinct netlink TLV attributes via separate print statements, so they appear on separate lines (e.g. `pair a code open circuit\npair a, fault length: 2.31m`). With no DOTALL, `.*?` cannot cross the newline, so finditer yields [] (reproduced exactly against the finding's evidence and a full 4-pair sample). `_real_cable_test` then returns {"status":"ok","faults":[]}, and at lines 115-116 `checks["cable_ok"] = ct.get("status") != "fault"` becomes True — a genuinely faulted cable is scored as a healthy link. That is a wrong PASS verdict on real hardware = critical by the project's own definition. Not already-handled: the mock path (_mock_cable_test) builds the dict directly and never exercises this regex, and there is no corpus capture replaying real TDR text, so no test covers it. It is inside a `# pragma: no cover - real-hw path`, but per project conventions a parsing bug inside such a path is a legitimate high-value finding, not an excused untested path. Additional corroborating defect: even when code+distance share a line, `(\d+)` captures only the post-decimal fragment, mis-parsing 2.31m as 31m — so the parser is wrong in both layouts. Suggested fix (line-by-line accumulate last pair/code, pair with the next fault-length line, capture `\d+(?:\.\d+)?` for distance, treat OK/Good Cable as non-fault, and add a real-output corpus replay) is appropriate.  

**C3. Guard-band check uses only the current GCL entry's end, not the contiguous open interval for the traffic class — false guard violations when a gate stays open across adjacent entries**  
`src/computetest/tsn/qbv.py` · src/computetest/tsn/qbv.py:108-110 (check_qbv_gate_timing) · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** A frame whose traffic-class gate is open continuously across two or more consecutive GCL entries is wrongly flagged as a guard-band violation if it finishes after the current entry ends, even though the gate for that TC never closes — producing a wrong FAIL verdict on a perfectly legal schedule.  
- **Evidence:** `_, _, w_end = schedule.window_at(start)` returns only the end of the single GCL entry covering `start`, then `if start + tx_ns > w_end:` flags a guard violation. In 802.1Qbv a GCL entry changes the full 8-bit gate vector at once, so a TC routinely stays open across multiple consecutive entries while other TCs toggle. Reproduced: schedule [GclEntry((1<<6)|(1<<0),100_000), GclEntry(1<<6,100_000)] (TC6 open continuously 0..200us); a TC6 frame at start=99_500 needing 1000ns ends at 100_500 while the TC6 gate is still open, yet `guard_violations -> [(6, 99500)]`, `ok=False`. The guard band only exists to suppress a frame that cannot finish before *its own gate* closes, not before an unrelated TC's state-change boundary.  
- **Fix:** Compute the frame's real deadline as the end of the contiguous run of entries for which `gate_states & (1<<tc)` is set, starting at the window covering `start` (walk forward, summing durations, until the first entry where the TC gate is closed; handle cycle wrap). Compare `start + tx_ns` against that gate-close time, not against the single entry's `w_end`.  
- **Verifier:** Independently reproduced at /Users/kywwilson/Desktop/Projects/Zoox/toolkit/src/computetest/tsn/qbv.py:107-110. The guard-band check does `_, _, w_end = schedule.window_at(start)` then `if start + tx_ns > w_end`, where `w_end` is the end of the SINGLE GCL entry covering `start`, not the close time of the frame's traffic-class gate.

In IEEE 802.1Qbv each GCL entry rewrites the entire 8-bit gate vector at once, so a traffic class routinely stays open across several consecutive entries while other TCs toggle. The guard band exists only to suppress a frame that cannot finish before ITS OWN gate closes — not before an unrelated internal state-change boundary. The module's own docstring agrees: "a frame that can't finish before its open window closes."

Reproduced exactly as claimed with schedule [GclEntry((1<<6)|(1<<0),100_000), GclEntry(1<<6,100_000)] (TC6 gate open continuously 0..200us). A TC6 frame at start=99_500 needing 1000ns ends at 100_500 while gate_open_at(100_500, 6) is still True, yet the code returns guard_violations=[(6, 99500)] and ok=False. I confirmed a correct contiguous-open-run deadline distinguishes this (legal) case from the genuinely-illegal case (schedule where TC6 truly closes at 100us), which the current code cannot.

Refutation attempts failed: (1) the existing test schedule _sched() has zero overlap between the two entries' gate vectors (entry0=TC6 only, entry1=TC0-5 only), so no TC ever straddles a boundary — the bug is structurally untestable by the current suite, and test_guard_band_violation only covers a TRUE close. (2) No schedule normalization/merge of contiguous identical gates exists. (3) No caller-side constraint requires frames to fit within one entry; check_qbv_gate_timing is a public API export. (4) This is core verdict logic, not a pragma real-hw path, and not honesty-discipline/mock-fidelity design.

Impact: a wrong FAIL (false guard-band violation) on a perfectly legal 802.1Qbv schedule — a wrong PASS/FAIL verdict, which the project rubric classifies as critical. (Direction is a false-FAIL rather than a false-PASS, but it still corrupts the verdict on real, legal hardware schedules.) Suggested fix (walk forward over consecutive entries while the TC bit stays set, summing durations with cycle-wrap handling, and compare start+tx_ns against that gate-close time) is sound.  

**C4. Sequence recovery ignores 16-bit R-TAG wrap-around -> false LOSS/FAIL on real hardware**  
`src/computetest/tsn/frer.py` · src/computetest/tsn/frer.py:33-39 (SequenceRecovery.receive) and check_frer · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** The window/staleness test uses plain monotonic integer comparison (seq <= _max - history_len) and a monotonic high-water mark, so when the IEEE 802.1CB R-TAG SequenceNumber (a 16-bit field, 7.8) wraps 65535 -> 0, the new wrapped frames are classified as 'stale' and eliminated, reporting loss that did not occur. A correct Vector Recovery Algorithm wraps modulo 2^SequenceNumberLength.  
- **Evidence:** Line 33: `if self._max is not None and seq <= self._max - self.history_len: return False`. Line 36: `self._max = ... max(self._max, seq)`. Demonstrated: after receiving [65533,65534,65535], the legitimate next frames 0,1,2 all return False (eliminated). Through check_frer with sent=[65534,65535,0,1] the verdict is `no_loss=False, exactly_once=False, ok=False` even though every distinct frame arrived once — a FALSE FAIL that would flag healthy redundant hardware as broken.  
- **Fix:** Model the sequence-number space explicitly: take a `seq_width` (default 16) / modulus, and compute the in-window test and high-water advance using modular distance (e.g. signed delta = ((seq - _max + half) % mod) - half) as 802.1CB 7.4.3.2 does, rather than raw integer <= and max(). At minimum, mask incoming seq to the configured width and handle wrap; add a wrapping-stream test.  
- **Verifier:** Confirmed and reproduced. In /Users/kywwilson/Desktop/Projects/Zoox/toolkit/src/computetest/tsn/frer.py, SequenceRecovery.receive uses pure monotonic integer logic: line 33 `if self._max is not None and seq <= self._max - self.history_len: return False` and line 36 `self._max = seq if self._max is None else max(self._max, seq)`. No modular arithmetic, no width mask, no wrap handling anywhere in the module (grep confirms zero references to 65535/2**16/0xFFFF/modulo/seq_width/mask in tsn/frer.py).

Reproduced the finding exactly: receiving [65533,65534,65535] then 0,1,2 returns False for all three wrapped frames; check_frer(sent_seqs=[65534,65535,0,1], arrivals=[65534,65535,0,1]) yields checks={'no_loss': False, 'no_duplicates_delivered': True, 'exactly_once': False}, ok=False. I also built a realistic clean redundant stream of 20 frames crossing the boundary (65530..65535, 0..13, each delivered twice): every distinct frame arrives exactly once, yet ok=False with 14 of 20 frames falsely classified as lost (delivered=[65530..65535]). That is a wrong FAIL on healthy fail-operational hardware.

Spec citation is accurate: IEEE 802.1CB R-TAG SequenceNumber is a 16-bit field (7.8) and the Vector/Match Recovery Algorithm operate on a sequence space that wraps modulo 2^SequenceNumberLength; modular distance, not raw integer compare, is the conforming behavior.

Adversarial refutation attempts all fail: (1) No guard/test covers wrap — test_tsn_frer.py only uses small monotonic sequences (0..3, 10..14) and never crosses 65535. (2) Not a real-hw pragma path, so the 'do not flag untested pragma path' convention does not apply. (3) Not intentional/honest-discipline: there is no documented scope-out, no scheduled-debt budget for FRER wrap, and the docs list FRER as Mandatory (docs/tsn/gptp_features_coverage_matrix.md CB-1). The docstring takes 'each R-TAG sequence number' (the raw 16-bit wire value) and claims to be '802.1CB vector/sequence recovery' — there is no contract requiring callers to pre-unwrap. A mock that fabricates loss that did not occur violates the stated 'faithfully model real dynamics' convention and lands in the explicitly-flag-worthy 'mislabels a verdict / claims a FAIL it cannot back' category.

Severity critical is correct: this yields a wrong FAIL verdict on real, healthy redundant hardware. A real validation run crosses the 16-bit wrap routinely (any meaningful frame count or a counter that simply starts mid-range), so the toolkit would flag conformant fail-operational hardware as broken. Suggested fix (mask incoming seq to a configured seq_width/modulus and use signed modular distance for both the in-window test and the high-water advance, plus a wrapping-stream test) is appropriate.  

**C5. ECC decoded from wrong SPD byte (DDR4 byte 13, not DDR5 byte 235) — wrong RAS verdict on real hardware**  
`src/computetest/ddr5/spd.py` · src/computetest/ddr5/spd.py:42 (parse_spd), ecc=bool(data[13] & 0x08) · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** On a real JESD400-5 DDR5 SPD, byte 13 (0x00D) is NOT the ECC/module-organization byte — it is SDRAM Duty-Cycle-Adjuster / Partial-Array-Self-Refresh / thermal-refresh options. The DDR5 ECC (bus width extension) lives in byte 235 (0xEB) bits [4:3] (00=no ECC, 01=8-bit ECC). Reading data[13] & 0x08 is the DDR4 Module-Organization layout, so the parser reports ECC presence from a byte that means something else entirely.  
- **Evidence:** Docstring claims 'JESD400-5 bytes 2/3/4/13' and the code does `ecc=bool(data[13] & 0x08)`. Public DDR5 SPD references place ECC/bus-width-extension at byte 235 (0xEB) bits 4-3 ('Bus extensions per sub-channel'); byte 13 in DDR5 is duty-cycle/refresh/thermal options. The hand-built corpus sets byte 13=0x08 so ecc decodes True, but that is the DDR4 location.  
- **Fix:** Decode ECC from byte 235 (0xEB): `ecc = ((data[235] >> 3) & 0x03) != 0` (bits[4:3] != 00 means an ECC bus-width extension is present), and raise/handle if the buffer is shorter than 236 bytes. Update the docstring and the corpus accordingly. Treating byte 13 bit 3 as ECC will mis-verdict the RAS 'ecc' check the conformance test exists to validate.  
- **Verifier:** Confirmed real defect at src/computetest/ddr5/spd.py:42. parse_spd decodes `ecc=bool(data[13] & 0x08)` with a docstring claiming "JESD400-5 bytes 2/3/4/13" (the DDR5 SPD spec). Verified against JEDEC/spec sources: on a real DDR5 (JESD400-5) SPD, byte 13 (0x0D) is the SDRAM Thermal/Refresh options byte (PASR/ODTS/ASR/ETR) — it has nothing to do with ECC. DDR5 ECC/bus-width-extension lives in byte 235 (0xEB), bits [4:3] ("Bus extensions per sub-channel": 00=no ECC). Reading data[13] bit 3 is a DDR4-era layout (DDR4 byte 13 = Module Memory Bus Width, carrying the ECC bus-width extension). So on real hardware the parser reports ECC presence from an unrelated thermal/refresh bit, producing a wrong `ecc_matches` RAS verdict in check_spd — the exact check the conformance test exists to validate.

Not already-handled: the only guard is `len(data) < 14`, which is sized for the wrong byte (reading byte 235 needs >= 236 bytes). Not intentional: the docstring explicitly claims JESD400-5, so this is not honest "unknown->None" handling or a deliberate DDR4 path; it claims spec conformance it cannot back. The tests pass only because the hand-built 16-byte corpus (spd.hex sets byte 13=0x08) and expected.json (ecc:true) both encode the same wrong byte — a self-consistent/circular fixture that cannot catch the real-hw bug (tests/test_ddr5_spd.py:29-34).

Severity critical per project convention: wrong PASS/FAIL (RAS ECC) verdict on real hardware. One minor inaccuracy in the finding's prose — it calls byte 13 the "DDR4 Module-Organization" byte, but DDR4 Module Organization is byte 12; byte 13 is DDR4 Module Memory Bus Width. This mislabel does not affect the substance: byte 13 is a DDR4-era ECC/bus-width location, and DDR5 puts ECC at byte 235. The suggested fix (decode bits [4:3] of byte 235, guard buffer length >= 236, fix docstring + corpus) is spec-correct.  

**C6. Type-1 cache-only device wrongly required to expose HDM-D[B] + BISnoop, yielding a false FAIL on conformant hardware**  
`src/computetest/cxl/device.py` · src/computetest/cxl/device.py:48-55 (check_coherency, coherency_bridging) · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** A spec-correct CXL Type-1 accelerator (CXL.cache only, no host-managed device memory) reports hdm_d_capable=False/bi_snoop_capable=False because HDM does not exist on a Type-1 device; check_coherency treats it as cache_capable and demands (hdm_d_capable and bi_snoop_capable), so coherency_bridging=False and the overall verdict is FAIL for a fully conformant device.  
- **Evidence:** cache_capable = device_type in (CxlDeviceType.TYPE1, CxlDeviceType.TYPE2); ... "coherency_bridging": ((hdm_d_capable and bi_snoop_capable) if cache_capable else True). Live run: check_coherency(device_type=TYPE1, claims_cache=True, claims_mem=False, hdm_d_capable=False, bi_snoop_capable=False) -> ok=False, checks={'cache_claim_consistent': True, 'mem_claim_consistent': True, 'coherency_bridging': False}. HDM-D/HDM-DB are properties of device-attached/host-managed device memory (Type 2, and Type 3 in CXL 3.0 BISnoop); a Type-1 device has no HDM, so the requirement is non-physical for Type 1.  
- **Fix:** Gate the HDM-D[B]/BISnoop requirement on device-coherent MEMORY, not on cache. Use mem_capable for cache+mem (i.e. require coherency bridging only for TYPE2), or add an explicit 'requires_device_coherent_mem = device_type == CxlDeviceType.TYPE2' predicate: "coherency_bridging": ((hdm_d_capable and bi_snoop_capable) if device_type == CxlDeviceType.TYPE2 else True). Update the docstring from 'a cache-capable device must expose HDM-D[B]' to 'a Type-2 (cache+device-memory) device must expose HDM-D[B] + BISnoop'.  
- **Verifier:** Independently reproduced and confirmed. Live run of check_coherency(device_type=TYPE1, claims_cache=True, claims_mem=False, hdm_d_capable=False, bi_snoop_capable=False) returns ok=False with checks={'cache_claim_consistent': True, 'mem_claim_consistent': True, 'coherency_bridging': False} — exactly the false FAIL the finding describes (src/computetest/cxl/device.py:48-55).

Spec correctness holds: HDM-D and HDM-DB are coherence modes for host-managed *device-attached* memory, and BISnoop uses the CXL.mem Back-Invalidate channel. A spec-conformant CXL Type-1 accelerator implements CXL.io + CXL.cache only, has NO device memory and NO CXL.mem, so it cannot and should not expose HDM-D[B] decoders or BISnoop. Gating coherency_bridging on cache_capable (which includes TYPE1) therefore demands a non-physical capability of Type-1 hardware. The codebase's own hdm.py defines HDM as 'Host-managed Device Memory,' confirming the conflation between cache coherence (D2H/H2D snoop, used by Type 1) and device-memory coherence (HDM-D[B]/BISnoop, used by Type 2 and, in CXL 3.0 HDM-DB, Type 3).

Refutation attempts failed: (1) No guard or caller constrains inputs — check_coherency is a public API export (cxl/__init__.py) called directly with values read off real hardware IDENTIFY+DVSEC; nothing prevents a Type-1 device with HDM/BI=False. (2) The only Type-1 test, test_type1_cache_only (tests/test_cxl_device.py:36-40), passes hdm_d_capable=True/bi_snoop_capable=True — itself an impossible configuration for a cache-only device — so it MASKS the bug rather than catching it; there is no test for the realistic Type-1 case (HDM/BI=False). All 8 tests pass, confirming the bug is uncaught. (3) Not a mock-fidelity, pragma-real-hw, or honesty-discipline matter: this is a pure spec-logic function that actively emits a wrong FAIL, not a refuse-to-pass.

Impact matches the project's critical rubric (wrong PASS/FAIL verdict on conformant hardware): a fully conformant Type-1 device gets failed. The suggested fix is directionally correct; the most spec-accurate predicate is mem_capable (device-coherent memory: TYPE2, and TYPE3 under CXL 3.0 HDM-DB) rather than cache_capable, and the docstring at device.py:6-7 should change from 'a cache-capable device must expose HDM-D[B]' to device-memory-coherent devices. Severity confirmed critical.  

**C7. Deming bootstrap: sxy==0 resamples inject spurious slope=0, masking real station disagreement (false PASS at n=3)**  
`src/computetest/msa/station_correlation.py` · deming_regression (lines 176-185) interacting with _deming_point (lines 227-230) · _correctness_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** At n=3 (the documented minimum), ~12% of bootstrap resamples hit the `if sxy == 0: return 0.0, my` early-return branch and are counted as legitimate fits with slope 0.0. These spurious zeros drag the 2.5th-percentile of the slope distribution down to 0.0, so a genuinely disagreeing station pair (e.g. one station reading 1.5x the other) yields a slope CI of (0.0, 1.5) that contains 1.0 and reports `agrees == True`.  
- **Evidence:** The try/except only catches division by 2*sxy: `try: s, i0 = _deming_point(bx, by, lambda_ratio) except ZeroDivisionError: continue`. But `_deming_point` returns BEFORE any division when degenerate: `if sxy == 0: ... return 0.0, my` (line 230), so it never raises and the (0.0, my) point is appended to slopes/intercepts. Reproduced: x=[2,4,6], y=[3,6,9] (true slope 1.5) gives slopeCI=(0.0, 1.5) and agrees=True with 117/1000 spurious zeros; the same scale factor correctly DISAGREES at n>=4.  
- **Fix:** Skip degenerate resamples instead of treating slope=0 as a fit. Either raise inside `_deming_point` on the sxy==0 branch (and let the existing `except ZeroDivisionError: continue` catch it), or add `if sxy == 0: continue` / filter sentinel results in the bootstrap loop. Also consider requiring a minimum count of valid resamples before reporting a CI.  
- **Verifier:** Confirmed and reproduced exactly. In deming_regression's bootstrap (src/computetest/msa/station_correlation.py:176-185), the guard `except ZeroDivisionError: continue` (line 182-183) is meant to skip degenerate resamples, but _deming_point returns `(0.0, my)` at line 230 BEFORE reaching the division at line 232 whenever `sxy == 0`. So degenerate resamples never raise — they are appended as legitimate slope=0.0 fits.

Reproduced: x=[2,4,6], y=[3,6,9] (true slope 1.5) -> ci95_slope=(0.0, 1.5), ci95_intercept=(0.0, 9.0), agrees=True, with 117/1000 spurious zeros. The degenerate rate is structural at n=3: I enumerated all 27 equally-likely resamples and exactly 3/27 = 11.1% have sxy=0 (the cases where all three indices collapse to one DUT). With ~111 zeros in a 1000-iteration bootstrap, the 2.5th-percentile of the slope distribution is pinned to 0.0, so the slope CI lower bound is always 0.0.

The impact is worse than the finding states: at n=3 the verdict is unconditionally a false PASS for ANY positive scale-factor disagreement — I tested 1.5x, 2x, 3x, and 5x and all four report agrees=True (ci95_slope = (0.0, factor)), because the point slope is >1 and the spurious zero pins the lower bound below 1.0, so the interval always straddles 1.0. The same 1.5x disagreement at n>=4 correctly reports agrees=False (ci95_slope=(1.5,1.5)). This propagates to the public API: station_correlation({'A':[3,6,9],'B':[2,4,6]}) reports StationPair.ok=True.

n=3 is the documented, explicitly-supported minimum (test_too_few_points_raises in tests/test_msa.py:180-182 only rejects n<3; the docstring/error says ">=3 paired observations"). The existing scale-factor and offset detection tests (test_pure_scale_factor_detected_in_slope, test_systematic_offset_detected_in_intercept) all use n>=8 and never exercise this gap, so the bug is in the untested region.

Not refuted by any convention: this is pure always-on statistical math (grep confirms no # pragma, no mock_mode, no Backend guard). It is not mock fidelity, not a real-hw pragma path, and not an honesty-discipline None/raise case. It is exactly the category the conventions say to flag — it fakes a PASS and mislabels a station-disagreement verdict. The suggested fix (raise inside the sxy==0 branch so the existing except catches it, or `if sxy == 0: continue`, and require a minimum count of valid resamples) is correct. Critical severity stands: wrong PASS/FAIL verdict on the station-agreement gate at the documented minimum sample size.  

**C8. ECC read from wrong byte — uses DDR4 byte-13 bit3 instead of DDR5 byte-235 bits[4:3]**  
`src/computetest/ddr5/spd.py` · spd.py:42 (ecc=bool(data[13] & 0x08)) · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** In JESD400-5 (DDR5) the ECC / bus-width-extension is in byte 235 (0xEB) bits[4:3] (ECC present when nonzero), not byte 13. data[13] & 0x08 is the DDR4 (JESD400-4) 'Module Memory Bus Width' ECC-extension bit applied to a DDR5 SPD. On real DDR5 hardware byte 13 is a different field, so the ecc verdict is meaningless/garbage.  
- **Evidence:** Module: `ecc=bool(data[13] & 0x08)`. memtest86plus parse_spd_ddr5: `sbyte = get_spd(slot_idx, 235); spdi->hasECC = (((sbyte >> 3) & 3) > 0);`. Web-confirmed: DDR5 SPD byte 235 bits[4:3] = bus width extension per sub-channel (ECC), bits[2:0] primary bus width. Byte 13 holds DRAM-type/legacy fields in DDR5, not bus width.  
- **Fix:** Read byte 235: require len(data) > 235, then ecc = bool((data[235] >> 3) & 0x03). Bump the length guard from >=14 to cover byte 235 (or return ecc=None/raise when the SPD is truncated rather than silently reading the wrong offset).  
- **Verifier:** Confirmed real defect. At /Users/kywwilson/Desktop/Projects/Zoox/toolkit/src/computetest/ddr5/spd.py:42, parse_spd derives ECC from `bool(data[13] & 0x08)`. This is the DDR4 layout. JESD400-5 (DDR5) places the bus-width/ECC field at byte 235 (0xEB), Section 11.11 "Memory Channel Bus Width": bits[4:3] = bus extension per sub-channel (ECC, present when nonzero), bits[2:0] = primary bus width (web-confirmed against JESD400-5D Table 104). DDR5 byte 13 (0x0D) is instead "SDRAM Duty Cycle Adjuster and Partial Array Self Refresh" — an unrelated field. So on real DDR5 SPD bytes read over SMBus/I3C from the SPD5118 hub, the `ecc` verdict reads garbage.

Not refuted by any guard or test: parse_spd only checks `len(data) < 14`; it never reads or length-guards byte 235, so the correct field is never consulted. Not a pragma real-hw path — parse_spd is a pure parser with no Mock/Real split that runs on real SPD bytes regardless of backend. Not intentional/mock-fidelity: the module docstring explicitly claims "JESD400-5" / "DDR5 SPD identity/RAS fields (... bytes 2/3/4/13)" and the conformance check verdicts `ecc`, so it asserts DDR5 spec compliance it cannot back.

The passing corpus/test actually corroborate the bug rather than refute it: corpus/ddr5_spd/synthetic-ddr5-32gb/spd.hex is only 16 bytes (byte 235 doesn't exist) with byte 13 = 0x08, and expected.json has ecc=true — a synthetic fixture hand-crafted to match the buggy byte-13 read. tests/test_ddr5_spd.py passes only because fixture and code share the same wrong offset; it would still pass if the code were wrong, exactly the "weak test" category. On a real DDR5 DIMM this yields a wrong ECC/non-ECC conformance verdict (a RAS-relevant property), i.e. a wrong PASS/FAIL on real hardware -> critical. Suggested fix is correct: require len(data) > 235 and read `ecc = bool((data[235] >> 3) & 0x03)`, raising/None on truncation rather than silently reading the wrong offset.  

**C9. NMP message-header byte packs every field in the wrong bit position**  
`src/computetest/nvme/mi/messages.py` · src/computetest/nvme/mi/messages.py:127-133 (encode_mi_request) and 154-156 (decode_mi_response) · _spec-conformance_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** The NMP byte layout (MT=bits0:3, CSI=bit4, SLOTID=bits5:6, bit7=0) contradicts the NVMe-MI spec / libnvme, where the header byte is ROR=bit7, NMIMT=bits5:3, CSI=bit0; a spec-conformant device would decode the message type, command-slot, and request/response role entirely wrong.  
- **Evidence:** Code: `nmp = (int(msg.message_type) & 0x0F) | ((msg.csi & 0x01) << 4) | ((msg.slot_id & 0x03) << 5)`. libnvme mi.c builds it as `hdr->nmp = (NVME_MI_ROR_REQ << 7) | (NVME_MI_MT_MI << 3) | (ep->csi & 1)`. Verified empirically: for message_type=NVME_MI_COMMAND(1), csi=1 the module emits NMP=0x51 (0b01010001); spec-correct value is 0x09 (0b00001001). A spec parser reads the module's 0x51 as NMIMT=bits5:3=010=2 (NVMe Admin Command) instead of 1 (NVMe-MI Command) — a misrouted command on real hardware.  
- **Fix:** Encode `nmp = (ror << 7) | ((int(message_type) & 0x0F) << 3) | (csi & 0x01)` and decode `message_type = (nmp >> 3) & 0x0F; csi = nmp & 0x01; ror = (nmp >> 7) & 0x01`. Update the docstrings at lines 14-19 and 38-40 accordingly. Drop the invented 2-bit slot field (see separate finding).  
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED. The NMP byte layout in src/computetest/nvme/mi/messages.py is spec-non-conformant in field positions and would misroute every NVMe-MI transaction against real hardware.

Code (line 132-133, encode): `nmp = (int(msg.message_type) & 0x0F) | ((msg.csi & 0x01) << 4) | ((msg.slot_id & 0x03) << 5)` => MT in bits0:3, CSI in bit4, SLOTID in bits5:6, bit7 forced 0. Decode (lines 154-156) mirrors this: `MessageType(nmp & 0x0F)`, `csi=(nmp>>4)&1`, `slot_id=(nmp>>5)&3`.

I independently verified the authoritative layout from libnvme src/nvme/mi.c (master): all three message classes build the byte as `hdr->nmp = (NVME_MI_ROR_REQ << 7) | (NVME_MI_MT_* << 3) | (ep->csi & 1)` and decode checks `nmp & (NVME_MI_ROR_RSP << 7)` and `nmp & 0x1` for the slot. So per spec: ROR=bit7, NMIMT starts at bit3, CSI=bit0. The module's CSI(bit4) and MT(bits0:3) placements are simply wrong, and there is no ROR bit at all (it is hardcoded to 0). The 2-bit slot_id field at bits5:6 is invented; the spec's command slot is the single CSI bit (bit0).

Demonstrated bidirectional breakage numerically:
- Encode: an NVMe-MI Command (mt=1) request emits byte 0x01. A spec device parses 0x01 as NMIMT=0 (Control Primitive), CSI=1 — misrouted. Spec-correct request byte is 0x08 (or 0x09 with csi=1).
- Decode: a real device MI response byte is 0x88 (ROR=1, MT=1). The module computes 0x88 & 0x0F = 8 and calls MessageType(8), which is not a defined enum value (valid: 0,1,2,4,5) -> __post_init__ raises MiMessageError('unknown MI message type'). Every real-device response would fail to decode.

This is NOT shielded by project conventions: it is the always-run framing layer that produces the actual on-wire bytes consumed by real MCTP bindings (mctp.py docstring: 'Real Aardvark / I3C bindings plug in by subclassing'), so its output must be spec-conformant. The only test (tests/test_nvme_mi.py TestMiMessageEncode.test_encode_decode_round_trip, lines 126-141) is self-referential: it round-trips encode->decode and never asserts the wire byte against the spec value, so it passes despite the code being spec-wrong — a weak test, not a guard. The top-of-module docstring (line 17, 'NMI MJD + CSI bits + Slot ID') and lines 39-40/127 also misdescribe the layout. Not mock fidelity, not honesty discipline, not a pragma real-hw path.

Minor imprecisions in the finding (do not change the conclusion): the cited 0x51 example actually requires slot_id=2 (the round-trip test value), not just csi=1 — with slot_id=0 the byte is 0x11; and the spec NMIMT field is 4 bits (bits6:3), not 3 (bits5:3) — but either way the decoded message type is not 1, so the command is still misrouted. Severity remains critical: wrong command routing on encode and guaranteed decode failure on response against any spec-conformant NVMe-MI device. Suggested fix in the finding (`nmp = (ror<<7) | ((mt & 0x0F)<<3) | (csi & 0x01)`; decode `mt=(nmp>>3)&0x0F; csi=nmp&0x01; ror=(nmp>>7)&0x01`; add a real ROR field; drop the invented 2-bit slot; fix docstrings; and add a test asserting the literal wire byte against the spec) is correct.  

**C10. self-test-log entry array is read with the wrong JSON key; a failing DST can silently read as PASS**  
`src/computetest/nvme/health.py` · health.py:173 (_real_self_test_log) · _real-hw-path_ · conf=medium · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** The parser reads the log-entry array via `raw.get("Self-test Log")` (hyphenated) with fallback to `"logs"`. nvme-cli's JSON emits the array under `"Self Test Log"` (space-separated) in current versions. If neither key matches, `entry` falls back to `[{}][0]` = {}, so `result` defaults to 0 and `passed` becomes True whenever `in_progress` is False — i.e. a FAILED self-test would be reported as PASS. Combined with the absence of any corpus capture for this command, this is unverified on real format.  
- **Evidence:** entry = (raw.get("Self-test Log") or raw.get("logs") or [{}])[0]  
- **Fix:** Add the actual nvme-cli key(s) to the fallback chain (e.g. `raw.get("Self Test Log") or raw.get("Self-test Log") or raw.get("logs")`) and, more importantly, capture a real `nvme self-test-log -o json` into corpus/ and add a replay test (as already done for smart-log) so this verdict path is exercised off-hardware.  
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, and the real defect is worse than the finding states. In _real_self_test_log (src/computetest/nvme/health.py:172-177) the parser reads keys that match NO version of nvme-cli's actual JSON output. I verified against nvme-cli source at v2.0, the pinned v2.8 (corpus/_drift/nvme-version.txt), v2.11, and master: the self-test-log JSON consistently uses array key "List of Valid Reports", "Current Device Self-Test Operation", "Current Device Self-Test Completion", and per-entry "Self test result". The code instead looks for: cur=raw.get("current_operation"), entry=raw.get("Self-test Log") or raw.get("logs"), result=entry.get("result", entry.get("Self Test Result")), percent=raw.get("completion"). Every one of these misses. So on real output: cur=0 -> in_progress=False; entry=[{}][0]={}; result=0; and passed=(not in_progress) and result==0 = True. A genuinely FAILED DST (e.g. "Self test result": 7) is silently decoded as passed=True. That flows to checks["self_test_passed"]=True (health.py:141) and into NvmeHealth.ok = all(checks.values()) (health.py:36) -> a defective drive reports PASS.

Refutation attempts failed: (1) No guard catches it — there is no validation that any expected key was present. (2) No test exercises it — tests/test_nvme.py self-test cases (lines 16-30) all use the mock path (self_test_log mock branch), and tests/test_parsers_corpus.py's stub (line 47) only serves smart-log/id-ctrl; there is NO self-test-log corpus capture (confirmed: corpus/nvme/<ver>/<model>/ holds only smart-log.json + id-ctrl.json). So the real key-parsing is wholly unverified. (3) Not intentional per conventions — this is a logic/parsing bug inside a "# pragma: no cover - real-hw path" block, which the conventions explicitly say IS a real high-value finding, and it fakes a PASS, violating the honesty discipline.

One factual correction to the finding: it claims the array key is "Self Test Log" (space) and the suggested fix adds that literal — but the actual nvme-cli key is "List of Valid Reports", so the proposed fix would NOT work. The correct fix must use "List of Valid Reports" for the array, "Current Device Self-Test Operation"/"Current Device Self-Test Completion" for op/completion, and "Self test result" for the entry result (note exact casing), AND add a real self-test-log corpus replay test. Severity raised from high to critical: per the project rubric, a wrong PASS verdict on real hardware is critical, and this silently passes a failed device self-test.  

### High (42)

**H1. Gen6+ post-FEC FBER never affects the verdict — catastrophic FEC failure still reports PASS**  
`src/computetest/bert.py` · bert.py:276-307 (run_bert) and bert.py:455-468 (run_conductor); fec read at lines 278/457 · _honesty_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** On a PCIe 6.0+ link, run_bert/run_conductor read FecStats and store pre_fec/post_fec/fber/burst in the result, but the verdict status is derived ONLY from AER counting plus idle checks; post_fec_flit_errors/fber_estimate are never compared to any target, so a link whose post-FEC FBER vastly exceeds the documented ~1e-6 target still returns status='pass' (CLI exit 0).  
- **Evidence:** verdict.status = status (line 277/456) is set from the AER loop; the very next line reads `fec = backend.read_fec_stats(...)` and `fec.fber_estimate` is only passed into BertResult as a report field (lines 304-306 / 465-467) — it is never used in any comparison. The module's own _GEN6_FEC_EXPLANATION says 'the post-FEC FLIT error rate is the actual reliability signal (FBER target ~1e-6)'. Empirically: a Gen6 mock with injected_post_fec_flit_rate=1e-2 (fber=0.00999, ~10000x the 1e-6 target) returns `verdict status: pass | ok: True` and 'CLI exit would be PASS'.  
- **Fix:** Feed FBER into the verdict when FEC stats are present: e.g. if fec is not None and fec.fber_estimate > <gen6 FBER target> (or post_fec_flit_errors imply BER>target at confidence), set verdict.status='fail' (or at minimum surface it through .ok/CLI exit), rather than only emitting a prose note. If the design truly intends AER-only verdicts on Gen6, change the docstring/explain text so it does not claim FBER is 'the actual reliability signal' while ignoring it for the pass/fail decision.  
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED. In bert.py, both run_bert (verdict finalized at lines 276-277; fec read at 278; fec only stored as report fields at 304-307) and run_conductor (455-456 / 457 / 465-468) derive verdict.status solely from the AER sequential-decision loop plus idle-fault checks. The ber module that computes the verdict has no notion of FBER. BertResult.status -> verdict.status, .ok -> status=='pass', and cli.py _verdict_exit maps pass->0; none consult fber_estimate or post_fec_flit_errors.

Empirically reproduced (PYTHONPATH=src): a Gen6 x8 mock with injected_post_fec_flit_rate=1e-2 (fber_estimate ~0.00997, ~10,000x the documented 1e-6 target) and zero injected AER ber, run with target_ber=1e-6, returns verdict.status='pass', result.status='pass', ok=True, CLI exit 0. run_conductor with a clean C-runner stub behaves identically (status='pass', fber~0.00999, post_fec=124920). The module's own _GEN6_FEC_EXPLANATION (bert.py:43) states 'the post-FEC FLIT error rate is the actual reliability signal (FBER target ~1e-6)', yet that signal is excluded from the pass/fail decision — exactly a 'claims spec compliance it cannot back' case the project conventions say to FLAG.

Refutation attempts failed: (1) The finding's literal target_ber=1e-12/short-window example actually FAILs, but for the wrong reason (unprovable AER target / takt-budget exhaustion), not FBER; the true defect reproduces cleanly with the provable target_ber=1e-6 the project's own tests use for Gen6. (2) No test guards it — test_bert_fec.py::test_gen6_post_fec_flits_drive_fber injects FBER ~1e-3 (1000x target) but only asserts fber_estimate > 0.0, never the verdict. (3) The OCP emitter does attach an fber_target<=1e-6 validator to the fec.fber_estimate measurement (ocpdiag.py:288), but the OCP step result (step_end(result.status)) and diagnosis (pcie.bert.{result.status}) are still the AER-only verdict, so this is a partial mitigation in the OCP output stream only, not in the toolkit's primary PASS/FAIL signal (.ok / CLI exit). (4) Not the honesty-discipline 'raise on unknown limit' pattern — the FBER target is KNOWN (hardcoded 1e-6) yet silently ignored.

Severity: high. This is a wrong-PASS on the documented primary Gen6 reliability metric — a catastrophic post-FEC link reports PASS / exit 0. I keep it at high rather than critical because (a) AER still latches FEC-uncorrectable FLITs as uncorrectable errors, providing a backstop for the worst case, and (b) the Gen6 FEC path is forward-looking (mock-only today, real read_fec_stats is a vendor pragma path). But the gap is concrete: a link whose FEC is failing-but-still-correcting-enough that AER doesn't latch uncorrectables passes while FBER is thousands of times over target, and the docstring/explain text overclaims FBER as 'the actual reliability signal' while the verdict ignores it. Suggested fix as stated: feed fber_estimate (or a confidence-bounded BER from post_fec_flit_errors) into the verdict against the known Gen6 FBER target, or correct the explanation text to stop claiming FBER is the reliability signal.  

**H2. AerSnapshot decodes Device-Status fallback bits with the AER table -> uncorrectable name list is empty on no-AER devices**  
`src/computetest/aer.py` · aer.py:77-87 (AerSnapshot.uncorrectable / .correctable) reached via snapshot() fallback at aer.py:106-111 · _correctness_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** When a device has no AER capability, snapshot() falls back to read_errors() (Device Status) and stores DEVSTA_NONFATAL(bit1)/DEVSTA_FATAL(bit2)/DEVSTA_CORR(bit0) raw values, but AerSnapshot.uncorrectable/.correctable always decode through the AER UNCORRECTABLE_BITS/CORRECTABLE_BITS tables, which do not contain bits 1/2 (uncorr starts at bit 4) and map bit 0 to the wrong meaning. has_uncorrectable is True (raw != 0) yet the decoded name list is empty.  
- **Evidence:** snapshot() fallback: `r = read_errors(backend, bdf); return AerSnapshot(bdf, None, r.correctable_raw, r.uncorrectable_raw)`. AerSnapshot.uncorrectable: `return decode_uncorrectable(self.uncorrectable_raw)` which uses UNCORRECTABLE_BITS (4,5,12..22). Reproduced: a no-AER device with a NonFatal Device-Status bit gives `uncorrectable_raw=2, has_uncorrectable=True, snap.uncorrectable=[]`. diagnostics.py:46-47 then emits `"uncorrectable: "` with no error type, and to_dict() line 84 emits an empty list, while ErrorReading (which carries a `source` field) decodes the same data correctly via DEVSTATUS_UNC_BITS.  
- **Fix:** Give AerSnapshot a `source: str` field (default 'aer') like ErrorReading and select the decode table on it (DEVSTATUS_*_BITS when source=='devstatus'); set source from read_errors().source in the snapshot() fallback. Alternatively have snapshot() build/return an ErrorReading-backed view so the coarse names ('NonFatalDetected'/'FatalDetected'/'CorrErrDetected') survive into reasons() and to_dict().  
- **Verifier:** Reproduced exactly as claimed. snapshot()'s no-AER fallback (aer.py:106-111) stores Device-Status-derived raw values into AerSnapshot, but AerSnapshot.uncorrectable/.correctable (aer.py:77-83) ALWAYS decode through the AER tables UNCORRECTABLE_BITS/CORRECTABLE_BITS. read_errors() devstatus branch (aer.py:219) yields uncorrectable_raw = ds & (DEVSTA_NONFATAL|DEVSTA_FATAL) = bits 1/2, but UNCORRECTABLE_BITS starts at bit 4, so the bits decode to nothing. Concrete repro on a no-AER device with an injected uncorrectable: uncorrectable_raw=2, has_uncorrectable=True, snap.uncorrectable=[]. Downstream impact verified: diagnostics.reasons() emits the literal string "uncorrectable: " with no error type; to_dict()['aer']['uncorrectable']=[]; and ocpdiag.py:339-340 iterates the empty list so NO aer.uncorrectable.* telemetry measurement is emitted at all. The parallel ErrorReading type (carrying a source field, used by the chain diagnostic and BERT) decodes the SAME data correctly as ['NonFatalDetected'] via DEVSTATUS_UNC_BITS, proving the correct behavior is already known and that AerSnapshot is the inconsistent path.

Tried hard to refute: (a) the two existing tests that touch this path (test_aer_edges.py:105 and test_diagnostics.py:126) only assert has_uncorrectable is True — neither checks the decoded name list, so the suite does not catch this. (b) The only assertion of the coarse names (NonFatalDetected) is on ErrorReading (test_aer_edges.py:68-69), not AerSnapshot. (c) Not intentional honesty-discipline: nothing here is a deliberate None-mask/raise; it is a silent misdecode. (d) Not a pragma real-hw exemption — it is shared decode logic exercised by the mock.

Severity: high, not critical. The top-level verdict is NOT wrong — status and the ocpdiag diagnosis both correctly come out 'fail' because they key off has_uncorrectable (raw != 0), so there is no false PASS and no verdict corruption. But it IS a real correctness defect on a common, real-hardware-relevant configuration (no-AER endpoints with a genuine NonFatal/Fatal fault): the error TYPE is silently dropped from both the human reason string and the machine-readable JSON/OCP-diag telemetry, defeating the module's stated purpose of identifying which layer failed. Wrong/empty decoded value plus a missing telemetry measurement maps to high; it sits at the lower edge of high only because the pass/fail verdict survives. Suggested fix is sound: give AerSnapshot a source field (default 'aer') and select DEVSTATUS_*_BITS when source=='devstatus', set from read_errors().source in the fallback.  

**H3. Chain segment with no error source is silently reported PASS, never 'skip'**  
`src/computetest/diagnostics.py` · diagnostics.py:245-252 (diagnose_chain segment loop) + ChainSegmentResult · _honesty_ · conf=high · verdict=confirmed  
- **Claim:** An upstream chain BDF that exposes neither AER nor Device Status (error_source=='none') is reported with status 'pass' even though its bit-error rate was never measurable — contradicting the module's stated honesty rule that a measurement you couldn't make is never a PASS, and violating the parity the endpoint BERT already keeps (it returns 'skip').  
- **Evidence:** r = aer.read_errors(backend, m.bdf, aer.error_source(backend, m.bdf)); ... cor = [n for _, n, _ in r.correctable]; unc = [n for _, n, _ in r.uncorrectable]; ok = not unc and len(cor) <= upstream_correctable_ok; segments.append(ChainSegmentResult(m.bdf, m.direction, cor, unc, "pass" if ok else "fail")). For source=='none', read_errors returns ErrorReading(0,0,'none') so cor=[]/unc=[] => ok=True => 'pass'. ChainSegmentResult.status is even documented as only "pass" | "fail" (line 140). Contrast bert.BertResult.status which returns 'skip' when aer_available is False.  
- **Fix:** Capture the source once per BDF; if it is 'none', mark the segment status 'skip' (add 'skip' to the documented status set) and surface a reason like 'no PCIe error source on <bdf>'. ChainDiagnostic.status should treat an all-skip chain as 'skip', not 'pass', mirroring PcieDiagnostic.status.  
- **Verifier:** Reproduced concretely. In diagnose_chain (src/computetest/diagnostics.py:245-252), each upstream segment is read via aer.read_errors(backend, m.bdf, aer.error_source(backend, m.bdf)). For a BDF with no PCIe error source (no AER ext-cap AND read_device_status() is None), aer.error_source returns "none" and read_errors returns ErrorReading(0,0,"none"), so cor=[]/unc=[] => ok = (not [] and 0 <= 0) = True => ChainSegmentResult.status = "pass". I built a switch board with the switch-upstream port at has_pcie_cap=False and empty _ext_caps; the segment reports status 'pass' (summary prints "OK"), and ChainDiagnostic.status returns 'pass' for the whole chain, even though that segment's BER was never measurable.

This directly violates the module's own documented honesty invariant. PcieDiagnostic.status (lines 36-42) and bert.BertResult.status (lines 84-85; run_bert lines 212-218) both return "skip" for the no-error-source case, with explicit comments "we never claim PASS from a measurement we couldn't actually measure" and "skip (no AER / Device Status source)". ChainSegmentResult.status is even documented as only "pass" | "fail" (line 140), and the word "skip" never appears anywhere in the chain segment/aggregation logic. There is no guard in diagnose_chain or analyze_chain that filters or skips members lacking an error source. So the per-segment path is the single place that breaks the parity the rest of the toolkit deliberately keeps, and per the project's honesty discipline ("DO flag any place that ... mislabels a verdict") this is an in-scope, real defect rather than intentional design or a pragma real-hw exemption (it runs on the mock and on real hardware identically).

Severity high is justified because it is a wrong-PASS verdict for an unmeasured measurement on real hardware. I keep it at high rather than critical/medium: the trigger (an upstream root/switch port exposing neither AER nor a readable Device Status) is uncommon in the field since virtually all PCIe ports implement Device Status, and even when one segment is unmeasurable the endpoint BERT still stresses the whole chain and dmesg monitoring still runs, so the chain is not entirely blind. The suggested fix is correct: capture the source once per BDF, mark status 'skip' (add 'skip' to the documented set) with a reason like "no PCIe error source on <bdf>", and have ChainDiagnostic.status treat an all-skip chain as 'skip', mirroring PcieDiagnostic.status and bert.
